for any other cocommutative comonoid
(X,\gamma_{X},\iota_{X})
and any morphism
f:X\to A

there is a unique a cocommutative comonoid morphism
f^{\bullet}:(X,\gamma_{X},\iota_{X})\to(A_{\bf i},\gamma_{A_{\bf i}},\iota_{A_{\bf i}})

making the following triangle commute in
\mathcal{C}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{\bf i}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!f^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

•

For each
B\in\operatorname{Ob}({\mathcal{C}})
there is a cocommutative cosemigroup

(B_{\bf r},\gamma_{B_{\bf r}})
such that there is a morphism
\varepsilon^{\bf r}_{B}:B_{\bf r}\to B

in
\mathcal{C}
such that for any cocommutative cosemigroup
(Y,\gamma_{Y})
and any morphism
g:Y\to B
in
\mathcal{C}

there is a unique a cocommutative cosemigroup morphism
g^{\bullet}:(Y,\gamma_{Y})\to(B_{\bf r},\gamma_{B_{\bf r}})

such that the analogous triangle commutes.

•

For every
C\in\mathcal{C}
there is a counital structure

(C_{\bf a},\iota_{C_{\bf a}})
and a morphism
\varepsilon^{\bf a}_{C}:C_{\bf a}\to C
such that
for each counital structure
(Z,\iota_{Z})
and for each arrow
h:Z\to C
in
\mathcal{C}

there is a unique
h^{\bullet}:(Z,\iota_{Z})\to(C_{\bf a},\iota_{C_{\bf a}})

such that the analogous triangle commutes.

Moreover, the following automatically holds as well. Let
A\in\operatorname{Ob}({\mathcal{C}})
such that there is a cocommutative comonoid
(A_{\bf i},\gamma_{A_{\bf i}},\iota_{A_{\bf i}})
,
then there are arrows
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}_{A}:A_{\bf i}\to A_{\bf r}
and
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ia}}_{A}:A_{\bf i}\to A_{\bf a}

such that the following triangle commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\bf r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\bf a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{\bf i}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ia}}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{\bf r}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{\bf a}_{A}}

Theorem 6.15
.

Every Lafont category induces a Cocteau adjunction.

Proof.

First of all, as it has been already noted in Theorem
3.19
,
the forgetful functors
\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}:{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
,
\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}:{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}

are strict monoidal and symmetric.

Further, one has
\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}\dashv\mathsf{R}_{\bf i}
,
\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}\dashv\mathsf{R}_{\bf r}

and
\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}\dashv\mathsf{R}_{\bf a}
, so the condition of Proposition
3.22

is satisfied, so those adjunctions are lifted to symmetric monoidal adjunction inducing a Cocteau adjunction
by Theorem
6.11
.
∎

Example 6.16
.

One can extract several curious examples of Lafont categories from presentably symmetric monoidal categories.
We refer the reader to
[
AR94
]
and
[
MP89
, Chapter 2]
for a more systematic study
of presentable categories. Let us recall the relavant concepts.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a locally small category, then
\mathcal{C}
is
presentable
if

\mathcal{C}
has all small colimits
\kappa
-directed colimits and there is a
set

S
of

\kappa
-compact objects that generate
\mathcal{C}
under
\kappa
-filtered colimits for some cardinal
\kappa
.
A symmetric monoidal category
(\mathcal{C},\otimes,\mathds{1})
is presentable if
\mathcal{C}
is presentable and it is an SMC
such that the tensor product operation
\otimes
as a bifunctor preserves all small colimits.

If
(\mathcal{C},\otimes,\mathds{1})
is a presentably symmetric monoidal category,
then
\mathcal{C}
is also closed, which follows from the Adjoint Functor theorem.
Moreover,
\mathcal{C}/\mathds{1}
,
{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{C})
and

{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C})
are presentable, one can show that similarly to
[
HM25
, Theorem 10]
.
Besides, the forgetful functors
\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}:{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
,

\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}:{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
and
\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}:\mathcal{C}/\mathds{1}\to\mathcal{C}

have right adjoints. Thus any presentably SMC
(\mathcal{C},\otimes,\mathds{1})
satisfies Definition
6.16
.

There are quite a few examples of categories satisfying this observation.
The first example is the category
R-
Mod
of modules over a commutative ring
R
.
Thus, the category
\operatorname{Vect}_{k}
of vector spaces over a field
k

and the category of Abelian groups viewed as
\mathbb{Z}
-modules are also Lafont categories.
The category of
R
-modules
R-
Mod
is equivalent to the category

{\bf QCoh}(\mathcal{O}(\operatorname{Spec}R))
of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}(X)
-modules over the Zariski space
\operatorname{Spec}(R)

[
Har13
, Corollary II.5.5]
, so
{\bf QCoh}(\mathcal{O}(\operatorname{Spec}R))

is a Lafont category. There are also several examples of presentably SMC’s (and thus Lafont) from algebraic topology
such as the category
{\bf Ch}(\mathcal{A})
of chain complexes in a Grothendieck Abelian category
\mathcal{A}

(see
[
Bek00
, Proposition 3.10]
). Thus all the aforementioned categories are models
of the system
{\bf SILL}(\lambda)_{3}
. There are already some results styding the semantics of (intuitionistic) linear logic
in structures from algebraic geometry as in
[
Mel22
]
, so our example demonstrate how close
algebraic geometry and linear logic are once more.

7
Representing Cocteau categories 2-categorically

One can strengthen the above result from Theorem
6.11
by using some 2-categorical techniques
and the formal theory of comonads.
In the previous section, we have proved that any particular Cocteau category induces a Cocteau adjunction,
but in this section, we consider the 2-category of
all
Cocteau categories and fully faithfully
embed it to the Eilenberg-Moore 2-category over a 2-comonad constructed a particular 2-category of strict functors.
This allow us extend the results from the previous section to functors and natural transformations of Cocteau categories.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
{\bf Cocteau}
denote the 2-category of Cocteau categories is defined as follows:

•

The class of 0-cells is the class of all Cocteau categories,

•

Let
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i},\bang_{\bf r},\bang_{\bf a},\mu_{\bf ir},\mu_{\bf ia})
and

(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ia})
be Cocteau categories,
the class of 1-cells
{\bf Cocteau}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
consist of strict symmetric monoidal functors

\mathsf{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
such that:

–

\mathsf{F}
induce symmetric lax monoidal comonad morphisms
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i})\Rightarrow(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i})
,

(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf r})\Rightarrow(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r})
and
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf a})\Rightarrow(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a})

with the components

\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{F}
,

\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf r}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}
and

\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf a}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}\mathsf{F}
respectively.

–

For each
A\in\operatorname{Ob}({\mathcal{C}})
and for
{\bf c}\in\{{\bf i},{\bf r}\}
and
{\bf w}\in\{{\bf i},{\bf a}\}
,
the functor
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf c}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf c}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf c}}

of the induced Eilenberg-Moore categories
preserves the copying arrow for each coalgebra
(A,h_{A})\in\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf c}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A\otimes\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A\otimes\mathsf{F}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf c}}(\gamma_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf c}}(A)}}

and the functor
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf w}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf w}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf w}}

preserves the deletion arrow as follows for each coalgebra
(A,h_{A})\in\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf w}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf w}}(\iota_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf w}}(A)}}

–

the following squares commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\mu_{\bf ir}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir}\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\mu_{\bf ia}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ia}\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}

(8)

•

Let
\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}:(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i},\bang_{\bf r},\bang_{\bf a},\mu_{\bf ir},\mu_{\bf ia})\to(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ia})

be 1-cells, the class
{\bf Cocteau}(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G})
of 2-cells from
\mathsf{F}
to
\mathsf{G}

consists of symmetric monoidal natural transformations
\theta:\mathsf{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{G}
inducing symmetric lax monoidal comonad morphisms

(\mathsf{F},\bang_{\bf i})\Rightarrow(\mathsf{G},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i})
,

(\mathsf{F},\bang_{\bf a})\Rightarrow(\mathsf{G},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a})
and

(\mathsf{F},\bang_{\bf r})\Rightarrow(\mathsf{G},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r})

Besides, the following condition is satisfied

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\mu_{\bf ir}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir}\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\mu_{\bf ia}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ia}\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf a}}

(9)

where
\theta_{\bf i}
is defined as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}\bang_{\bf i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\bang_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\theta}

and
\theta_{\bf a}
and
\theta_{\bf r}
are defined similarly.

Prima facie, the reader might find the definition of 2-cells incomplete since 1-cells induce strict functors of Eilenberg-Moore categories that preserve the copying and deletion
operations, but it is not reflected at the level of 2-cells. Let
\theta:\mathsf{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{G}
be a 2-cell
in
{\bf Cocteau}
.
\mathsf{F}
and
\mathsf{G}
induce comonad morphisms
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf r})\Rightarrow(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r})
,
so we have the functors of Eilenberg-Moore categories
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}

and
\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}

defined as
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}:(A,h_{A})\mapsto(\mathsf{F}A,{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}_{A}\circ\mathsf{F}(h_{A}))

and
\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}:(A,h_{A})\mapsto(\mathsf{G}A,{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}_{A}\circ\mathsf{G}(h_{A}))
.
So
\theta:\mathsf{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{G}
extends to
\widehat{\theta}:\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}\Rightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}

and
\widehat{\theta}
is a strict symmetric monoidal natural transformation,
so the following diagram automatically commutes for each coalgebra
(A,h_{A})\in\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A\otimes\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}(A\otimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}A\otimes\mathsf{G}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}(A\otimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}{(\gamma_{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta}_{A}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta}_{A\otimes A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}{(\gamma_{A})}}

To proceed further, let us instantiate Theorem
3.13
the following way.

Theorem 7.2
.

{\bf Cmd}({\bf SymmMonCat})
, the 2-category of symmetric lax monoidal comonads over SMCs, their symmetric lax monoidal morphisms and transformations,
fully faithfully embeds to
{\bf Adj}^{l}({\bf SymmMonCat})
, the category of symmetric monoidal adjunctions in
{\bf SymmMonCat}
,
their left morphisms and modifications.

Now we would like to fully faithfully embed the whole 2-category
{\bf Cocteau}
to some 2-category of 2-functors, their strict natural transformations
and modifications similarly to Theorem
7.2
, but we would also like to reflect all the required connections between the constituent symmetric lax monoidal comonads in
Cocteau categories. So we have to provide a plausible candidate instead of
{\bf Adj}^{l}({\bf SymmMonCat})
, a subcategory of
{\bf Fun}([1],{\bf SymmMonCat})

spanned by those 1-cells in
{\bf SymmMonCat}
that have a right adjoint. Recall that one can unveil any Cocteau category as a Cocteau adjunction by Theorem
6.11
and left adjoints
in those adjunctions are forgetful functors from the Eilenberg-Moore category to the underlying SMC. Recall
that those functors are strict symmetric. So we take

{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
as a 2-subcategory of
{\bf SymmMonCat}
where left adjoints we are interested in
belong to as 1-cells.

Further, we have got to replace
[1]
with something more approritate since we have three comonads and transformations
betweem them.

Definition 7.3
.

The 1-category
{\bf FCocteau}
(read as “free Cocteau”) with four objects

\frownie,\smiley_{0},\smiley_{1},\smiley_{2}
and with morphisms generated by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smiley_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smiley_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frownie}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smiley_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{12}}

Now let us consider the 2-category
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
, where
we choose symmetric monoidal categories
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and
strict symmetric monoidal functors such that the following triangle commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}}

(10)

Further, we shall be using capital Gothic letters
\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B},\ldots

to range over elements of

{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
.

Triangles as in (
10
) reflect how left adjoints commute with one another, but we also
expect
\mathcal{D}_{0}
,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
to be relevant, Cartesian and semicartesian respectively. But
those triangles bear no information about that.
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
are not really problematic to us
since an SMC
\mathcal{C}
is Cartesian (semicartesian) iff the forgetful functor
from the category of cocommutative comonoids (from the slice category over
\mathds{1}
) is an isomorphism.
As we know from Theorem
5.4
, an SMC is relevant iff it forms a coalgebra in the
Eilenberg-Moore category
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{str}}^{\bf coSem}
. So our idea is to consider
Cocteau categories as coalgebras from the Eilenberg-Moore 2-category

({\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}))^{\mathsf{K}}
for a strict 2-comonad
inheriting the corresponding coalgebra from
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{str}}^{\bf coSem}
from Theorem
5.4
.
So recall that:

Definition 7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a 2-category and let
\mathsf{K}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
be a strict 2-functor,
then a (strict) 2-comonad is a triple
(\mathsf{K},\delta,\varepsilon)
, where

\delta
and
\varepsilon
are strict 2-natural transformations satisfying the coassociative and coidentity laws.

There is a more detailed destriction of (lax) 2-monads in
[
JY21
, §6.5]
,
so one can restore the details of the definitions for strict comonads quite easily. If we have a comonad
\mathsf{K}
over a 2-category
\mathcal{C}
, then we can associate the
Eilenberg-Moore 2-category

\mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{K}}
of strict coalgebras
(A,h_{A})
, morphisms and 2-cells.

The rest is to construct a desired 2-functor
\mathsf{K}
.
First of all, recall that the forgetful functors
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}:{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})\to\mathcal{D}_{0}
,

{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathsf{D_{1}}}:{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\to\mathcal{D}_{1}

and
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}:\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}\to\mathcal{D}_{2}
are strict symmetric, so they
are 1-cells in the category
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
. So let us postcompose (
10
)
with the functors as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathsf{D_{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathds{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}}

(11)

where the functors
\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}
and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}
are the following composites respectively:

{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\xrightarrow{{\bf coSem}(\mathsf{U}_{10})}{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})

{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ia}}\mathcal{D}_{1}/\mathds{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{U}_{12}/\mathds{1}}\mathcal{D}_{0}/\mathds{1}

where
\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}:{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\to{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
is a strict symmetric monoidal functor
dropping a counit from every cocommutative comonoid in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
making it a cosemigroup
and
\mathsf{U}_{\bf ia}:{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\to\mathcal{D}_{1}/\mathds{1}

is a strict symmetric monoidal functor that takes a cocommutative comonoid in
\mathcal{D}_{1}

and returns its counit. It is readily checked that
\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}
and
\mathsf{U}_{\bf ia}

are strict as well as
\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}
and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}
.
So all the morphisms in (
11
) are 1-cells in
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

1.

The mapping
\mathsf{K}
that takes
\mathfrak{A}\in{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})

and returns the triangle of the form of (
11
) is a 2-endofunctor on
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
.

2.

\mathsf{K}
is a strict 2-comonad over the 2-category
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
.

Proof.

1.

Let
f:\mathfrak{A}\to\mathfrak{B}
be a 1-cell in
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
given by the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{1}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}}

Thus

is given by:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}

(12)

where

is a functor sending a cocommutative comonoid
in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
to a cocommutative comonoid in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and this functor does exist since

\mathsf{H}_{1}
is strict monoidal, see
[

, Proposition 3.38]
.

\widehat{\mathsf{H}_{2}}:\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}/\mathds{1}
is a functor of slice categories extending
\mathsf{H}_{2}
,
which is also strict monoidal.
\widehat{\mathsf{H}_{0}}:{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})\to{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{0})
is defined similarly
to
\widehat{\mathsf{H}_{1}}
. Checking functoriality for 1-cells is routine.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{H}_{2}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}^{\prime}}

3.

We have got to define the counit and comultiplication and check the axioms of 2-comonad.

The counit natural transformation
\varepsilon:\mathsf{K}\Rightarrow{\bf Id}
consists of the components

\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{A}}:\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{A}\to\mathfrak{A}
for
\mathfrak{A}\in{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
that
take a diagram as in (
11
) and transforms it into a diagram as in (
10
)
by the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{V}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathsf{D}_{1}}}

We have already noticed above that
\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{A}
is a 1-cell in
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
,

\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{A}}
is well-defined for each
\mathfrak{A}
.

4.

The comultiplication natural transformation
\delta:\mathsf{K}\Rightarrow\mathsf{K}\mathsf{K}

with the components
\delta_{\mathfrak{A}}:\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{A}\to\mathsf{K}\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{A}

for each
\mathfrak{A}
given by a quadruple of functors
(\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}},\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}},\delta^{\bf a}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}},1_{\mathcal{C}})

making the below diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}/\mathds{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf a}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}_{{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1}),\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1}),{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}}

where
\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}
is comultiplication of the
{\bf coSem}
-comonad
from Lemma
5.3
.
\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}:{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})\to{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})

and
\delta^{\bf a}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}:\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}\to(\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}/)\mathds{1}
are functors defined similarly to

\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}
. Let us show that the above diagram commutes indeed.

The morphisms
\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}
,
\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
and
\delta^{\bf a}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}
are
strict indeed:
\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
establishes an isomorphism
between
{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
and
{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
by
[
Mel09
, Corollary 19]

(and the same consideration is applicable to
\delta^{\bf a}_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}
), whereas
\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}
is strict
symmetric as it follows from Lemma
5.3
.

So we take the diagram from (
11
), which is already commutative and precompose it with the forgeful functors

{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{0})
to
{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})
, from
{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
to
{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})

and from
\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}/\mathds{1}
to
\mathcal{D}_{2}/\mathds{1}
, so the resulting bigger triangle also commutes. Now consider the following square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1}),{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}}

that we unveil into the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf i}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathsf{U}_{10})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\bf r}_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}^{2}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathsf{U}_{10})}

where
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}^{2}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}({\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}^{2}((\mathcal{D}_{1}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}_{{\bf coMon(\mathcal{D}_{1})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}({\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}})}

The left-hand side square commutes immediately, whereas the right-hand side square commutes by Lemma
5.3
. Checking
that
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
,
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf ir}}^{2}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
,
{\bf coSem}(\mathsf{U}_{10})

and
{\bf coSem}^{2}(\mathsf{U}_{10})
are 1-cells in
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
is routine.
The axioms of a 2-comonad follow from Theorem
5.3
.

∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i},\bang_{\bf r},\bang_{\bf a},\mu_{\bf ir},\mu_{\bf ia})

be a Cocteau category.
Then there is a
\mathsf{K}
-coalgebra from the Eilenberg-Moore 2-category
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})^{\mathsf{K}}

associated with
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

As far as
\mathcal{C}
is a Cocteau category, so, by Theorem
6.11
, we have the following commutative diagram
of left adjoints of the corresponding Cocteau adjunction:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{l}}

(13)

where
\mathsf{r}
and
\mathsf{a}
are also strict symmetric. Thus (
13
) is a 1-cell in

{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
, that we will further denote as
\mathfrak{A}
.
To make
\mathfrak{A}
a coalgebra, we have got to provide the coalgebra action
h_{\mathfrak{A}}:\mathfrak{A}\to\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{A}
,
which is given by:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}},\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}},\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}}

The above diagram commutes indeed since
\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}
(
\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}
)
is already Cartesian (semicartesian) isomorphic to the category of its cocommutative comonoids (
\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}
)
via the corresponding forgetful functor,
whereas
\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}
is relevant by Lemma
6.7
,
so the forgetful functor
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
has a strict section
{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
.

The coalgebra conditions for
h_{\mathfrak{A}}
follow from Theorem
5.4
.
∎

Before formulating the final theorem, let us note that all the functors between Cocteau categories
we consider are strict as monoidal functors, whereas in Theorem
7.2
all 1-cells are lax by default.
So we formulate the following proposition refining that theorem:

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be SMCs and let
\mathsf{S}
and
\mathsf{T}
be symmetric lax monoidal comonads on

\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
respectively. Let
\mathsf{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a symmetric lax monoidal functor
inducing a comonad morphsim
(\mathcal{C},\mathsf{S})\to(\mathcal{D},\mathsf{T})
, then

\mathsf{F}
is strict if and only if
\mathsf{F}:\mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{S}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{T}}
is strict.

In the above lemma, we associated a
\mathsf{K}
-coalgebra with every Cocteau category.
However, the 2-category
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
has too many triangles, so we take only those coalgebras whose functors
\mathsf{F}_{0}
,
\mathsf{F}_{1}
and

\mathsf{F}_{2}
(the choice of names as in
10
) admit right adjoints: since they might not exist apriori in a general case,
but we need them unveil comonads from Cocteau categories. Let
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})_{R}^{\mathsf{K}}

denote such a 2-subcategory. Then we have the following.

Theorem 7.8
.

{\bf Cocteau}
fully faithfully embeds to
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})_{R}^{\mathsf{K}}
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i},\bang_{\bf r},\bang_{\bf a},\mu_{\bf ir},\mu_{\bf ia})

be a Cocteau category. As it has been already discussed in Lemma
7.6
,
we have a
\mathsf{K}
-coalgebra
(\mathcal{\mathfrak{C}},h_{\mathfrak{C}})
,
where
\mathfrak{C}
is a 0-cell of
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})

as in (
13
). Let us fix that
\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
,
\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
and
\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}

(we will parametrise those functors with the underlying SMC further) already have right adjoints
by Theorem
6.11
and this will allow us to stay within
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})_{R}^{\mathsf{K}}
all the time,
so let us fix it here.

Let us first show that a strict symmetric monoidal functor of Cocteau categories
\mathsf{F}\in{\bf Cocteau}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

extends to a coalgebra morphism
\mathsf{F}:(\mathfrak{C},h_{\mathfrak{C}})\to(\mathfrak{D},h_{\mathfrak{D}})
,
where
(\mathcal{\mathfrak{D}},h_{\mathfrak{D}})
is a
\mathsf{K}
-coalgebra obtained by applying
Lemma
7.6
to
\mathcal{D}
. In other words, we need to construct a 1-cell

\mathsf{F}:\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{D}
in
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
making the following square commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\mathfrak{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}\mathsf{F}}

(14)

\mathsf{F}:\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{D}
, in turn, is given by the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}

In other words, a 1-cell
\mathsf{F}
is given by a quadruple
of functors
(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}},\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}},\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}},\mathsf{F})
,
where the
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}
,
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}
and
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}

are functors of the corresponding Eilenberg-Moore categories obtained from the components of comonad morphisms

\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf r}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}
,

\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf i}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}\mathsf{F}

and
\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf a}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}\mathsf{F}
respectively.

Whereas
\mathsf{K}\mathsf{F}:\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{C}\to\mathsf{K}\mathfrak{D}
is obtained by instantiating parameters properly
in (
12
). However, it is could be useful the reader to have an explicit visualisation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}^{\bang_{\bf i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}})}

(15)

So we have a 1-cell given by a quadruple:

({\bf coSem}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}})\circ{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}},{\bf coMon}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}})\circ{\mathsf{V}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}},(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}})/\mathds{1}\circ{\mathsf{V}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}},\mathsf{F})

(16)

where
{\mathsf{V}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
and
{\mathsf{V}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}

are inverses to the forgetful functors
{\mathsf{U}_{\bf i}}_{{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}}
and

{\mathsf{U}_{\bf a}}_{{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}}
respectively.

{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
is also a coalgebra action, so the following diagrams
commute in
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}/\mathds{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coMon}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}})/\mathds{1}}

Therefore, we have:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\mathsf{K}\widehat{\mathsf{F}}\circ h_{\mathfrak{C}}=({\bf coSem}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}})\circ{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}},{\bf coMon}(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}})\circ{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}},(\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}})/\mathds{1}\circ{\mathsf{V}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}},\mathsf{F})=&\\
&\>\>\>\>\>\>\>\>({\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}\circ\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}},{\mathsf{V}_{\bf i}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}\circ\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}},{\mathsf{V}_{\bf a}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}\circ\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}},\mathsf{F})=\widehat{\mathsf{F}}\circ h_{\mathfrak{D}}&\end{array}

Checking that the mapping
\mathsf{F}\mapsto\widehat{F}
is functorial is immediate.

Now let
\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}\in{\bf Cocteau}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
and let

\theta:\mathsf{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{G}
be a 2-cell in
{\bf Cocteau}
. One needs to construct
a 2-cell
\hat{\theta}:\widehat{\mathsf{F}}\Rightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{G}}
in the 2-category

{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})_{R}^{\mathsf{K}}
.
As we have already showed above,
\widehat{\mathsf{F}}
and
\widehat{\mathsf{G}}
are already 1-cells in

{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})_{R}^{\mathsf{K}}
.

So we first need to check that the following diagram commutes in
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{10}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}_{12}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta_{\bf i}}}

(17)

where
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{s}}
and
\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{s}}
for
s\in\{{\bf i},{\bf r},{\bf a}\}
are the
induced functors of the Eilenberg-Moore categories given as follows for
(A,h_{A})\in\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{s}}
:

\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{s}}:(A,h_{A})\mapsto(\mathsf{F}A,{\mathsf{F}_{s}}_{A}\circ h_{A})

\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{s}}:(A,h_{A})\mapsto(\mathsf{G}A,{\mathsf{G}_{s}}_{A}\circ h_{A})

and both
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{s}}
and
\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{s}}
are strict symmetric since
\mathsf{F}
and
\mathsf{G}
are.
Further, the following square (and the corresponding ones for
{\bf r}
and
{\bf a}
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

commutes by Theorem
7.2
and Proposition
7.7
, so does (
17
).
Checking that (
17
) respects the structure of
\mathsf{K}
-coalgebras is routine.
The
\mathsf{C}
be a 2-functor mapping each Cocteau category to the coalgebra
(\mathfrak{C},h_{\mathfrak{C}})
.
To complete the proof of the theorem we must show that
The functor
\mathsf{C}:\mathcal{C}\mapsto
is fully faithful on 1-cells and
2-cells.
Let
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i},\bang_{\bf r},\bang_{\bf a},\mu_{\bf ir},\mu_{\bf ia})

and
(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ia})

be Cocteau categories and let
\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be 1-cells in
{\bf Cocteau}
such that
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{F})=\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{G})
.

We need
\mathsf{F}=\mathsf{G}
as the equality of 1-cells in
{\bf Cocteau}
, that
is, the equality of underlying functors
\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

as well as the equality of the induced symmetric monoidal comonad morphisms.

\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{F})=\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{G})
, in turn, means, the following equalities are the case:

\mathsf{F}=\mathsf{G}
,
\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\bf i}=\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}
,

\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\bf r}=\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}
and

\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\bf a}=\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf a}}
.
The equality
\mathsf{F}=\mathsf{G}
is already satisfied, whereas we have
\mathsf{F}_{s}=\mathsf{G}_{s}
for
s\in\{{\bf i},{\bf r},{\bf a}\}

by Theorem
7.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be a Cocteau categories, so we have coalgebras

(\mathfrak{C},h_{\mathfrak{C}})
and
(\mathfrak{D},h_{\mathfrak{D}})

as in Lemma
7.6
. Let
\mathfrak{F}:\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{D}
be a coalgebra morphism.
As a 1-cell in
{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
,
\mathfrak{F}
is given as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{l}}

(18)

We must show that
\mathsf{F}
provides a 1-cell in
{\bf Cocteau}
, that is,
\mathsf{F}
satisfies Definition
7.1
.
Consider the case of exponential and relevant comonads, the affine part is considered similarly.

First of all, the following square commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}

so, by Theorem
7.2
, Proposition
3.22
and Proposition
7.7
, we have a symmetric lax monoidal comonad morphism
(\mathsf{F},\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{0}}):(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf r})\Rightarrow(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r})

with the component
\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{0}}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{\bf r}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}
induced by
\mathsf{F}_{0}
.
So we let
\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}:=\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{0}}
. Similarly, we get
\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}:=\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{1}}
and

\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}:=\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{2}}
.

Let us check the rest of the conditions.

1.

Let us check that (
8
) is satisfied.

On the one hand, we have the following left morphism of adjunctions:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}

which gives us the comonad morphism
\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir}\mathsf{F}\circ\mathsf{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}
by Theorem
7.2
, Proposition
3.22
and Proposition
7.7
.

On the other hand, we have:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}

which, in turn, induces the comonad morphism
\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}\circ\mathsf{F}\mu_{\bf ir}
by the same statements.
Finally, the following square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}_{0}}

commutes by (
18
), and, thus, the composites
\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}\circ\mathsf{F}\mu_{\bf ir}
and

\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir}\mathsf{F}\circ\mathsf{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}
are equal.

2.

Now let us check that the functor

\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}
(which coincides with

\mathsf{F}_{0}
) preserves diagonals.
The following square already commutes by the condition that
\mathfrak{F}
is a
\mathsf{K}
-coalgebra morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{V}_{\bf r}}_{\mathcal{D}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf coSem}(\mathsf{F}_{0})}

(19)

Let us discuss what each of the composites does.
Let
(A,h_{A})
be a coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}
. Then we have a coalgebra

(\mathsf{F}A,{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}_{A}\circ\mathsf{F}(h_{A}))
in
\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}
.
Finally, we endow the
(\mathsf{F}A,{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}_{A}\circ\mathsf{F}(h_{A}))
with the comultiplication
operation
\gamma_{\mathsf{F}A}:\mathsf{F}A\to\mathsf{F}A\otimes\mathsf{F}A
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}(\bang_{\bf r}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}A\otimes\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}\mathsf{F}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}A\otimes\mathsf{F}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}(h_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{c}^{\prime\bf r}_{\mathsf{F}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{\bf r}_{\mathsf{F}A}\otimes\varepsilon^{\bf r}_{\mathsf{F}A}}

(20)

On the other hand, we take
(A,h_{A})
in
\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}
and endow it with
the comultiplication operation
\gamma_{A}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang_{\bf r}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bang_{\bf r}A\otimes\bang_{\bf r}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{c}^{\bf r}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon^{\bf r}_{A}\otimes\varepsilon^{\bf r}_{A}}

Further, we take a cosemigroup
(A,h_{A},\gamma_{A})
and transform it to a cosemigroup in
\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}
as follows.
The carrier is a coalgebra
(\mathsf{F}A,{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}_{A}\circ\mathsf{F}(h_{A}))
with the comultiplication operation

\mathsf{F}(\gamma_{A})
.
But
\mathsf{F}(\gamma_{A})=\gamma_{\mathsf{F}A}
by (
19
).

3.

Let us show that
\mathcal{C}
is full and faithful on 2-cells. Let
\mathsf{G},\mathsf{H}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be functors of Cocteau categories satisfying Definition
7.1

and let
\theta,\theta^{\prime}:\mathsf{G}\Rightarrow\mathsf{H}
be natural transformations
such that
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in{\bf Cocteau}(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{H})
and

\mathsf{C}(\theta)=\mathsf{C}(\theta^{\prime})
.
We need
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
to equal as 2-cells in
{\bf Cocteau}
.

\mathsf{C}(\theta)=\mathsf{C}(\theta^{\prime})
means that there are the following equalities already satisfied.
We have
\theta=\theta^{\prime}
as the equality of 2-cells in
{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}}
.
And we also have
\widehat{\theta_{\bf i}}=\widehat{\theta^{\prime}_{\bf i}}
,

\widehat{\theta_{\bf r}}=\widehat{\theta^{\prime}_{\bf r}}
and
\widehat{\theta_{\bf a}}=\widehat{\theta^{\prime}_{\bf a}}
. But we conclude

\theta_{\bf i}=\theta^{\prime}_{\bf i}
,

\theta_{\bf r}=\theta^{\prime}_{\bf r}
and
\theta_{\bf a}=\theta^{\prime}_{\bf a}
from Theorem
7.2
proving that
all those
\theta
’s are symmetric lax monoidal comonad transformations.

The faithfulness part is slightly more complicated. Let
(\mathcal{C},\bang_{\bf i},\bang_{\bf r},\bang_{\bf a},\mu_{\bf ir},\mu_{\bf ia})

and
(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r},\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ir},\mu^{\prime}_{\bf ia})
be Cocteau categories
and let
\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}\in{\bf Cocteau}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
,
so
\mathsf{F}
and
\mathsf{G}
induce coalgebra morphisms

\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}:(\mathfrak{C},h_{\mathfrak{C}})\to(\mathfrak{D},h_{\mathfrak{D}})

and let
\theta:\mathsf{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{G}
be a 2-cell in

{\bf Fun}({\bf FCocteau},{\bf SymmMonCat}_{\operatorname{strict}})
given as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{a}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf i}}

(21)

Apriori
\theta
is a 2-cell in
{\bf SymmMonCat}
, but we have got to show that
\theta
provides a 2-cell in
{\bf Cocteau}
.
First of all, consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

(22)

We already know that
\mathsf{F}
and
\mathsf{G}
are equipped with symmetric lax monoidal comonad morphisms

(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{F}_{s}):(\mathcal{C},\bang_{s})\to(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{s})

and
(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{G}_{s}):(\mathcal{C},\bang_{s})\to(\mathcal{D},\bang^{\prime}_{s})
respectively
with the components
\mathsf{F}_{s}:\mathsf{F}\bang_{s}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{s}\mathsf{F}

and
\mathsf{G}_{s}:\mathsf{G}\bang_{s}\Rightarrow\bang^{\prime}_{s}\mathsf{G}
for
s\in\{{\bf i},{\bf r},{\bf a}\}
.
By Theorem
7.2
, Proposition
3.22
and Proposition
7.7
, therefore
\theta
induces symmetric lax monoidal comonad morphism transformation

(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{F}_{s})\Rightarrow(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{G}_{s})
.
To finally check (
9
), observe that the following square already commutes by (
21
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\bf r}}

(23)

Now consider the following prism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\bang_{\bf r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\bang^{\prime}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta_{\bf i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{F}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathsf{G}_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\theta_{\bf r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}\>\>\>\>\>\>\>}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}\>\>\>\>\>\>\>}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{C}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{I}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{R}^{\mathcal{D}}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{r}^{\prime}}

(24)

where laterial triangles commute since
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
induce Cocteau adjunctions by Theorem
6.11
,
the front square already commutes since it is (
23
), whereas the other two squares commute by the condition as well. Thus, we conclude
that (
9
) by applying Theorem
7.2
, Proposition
3.22
and Proposition
7.7
.

∎

8
Further Work

There are several question that we would like to leave for further investigation.

As regards proof theory, we have proved the strong normalisation property the
\beta
-reduction relation only,
but the strong normalisation for all proof normalisation rules is a problem requiring separate research. We
conjucture that one can develop a technique from
[
Tro95
, §8]
or
[
Tro92
, §21]
.

In Theorem
7.8
, we fully faithfully embedded the category of Cocteau categories to the 2-category
of functors and thier lax natural transformations, but this technique might require a further generalisation for an arbitrary
subexponential signature and the category of all assemblages.

[
Hos07
]
introduces relational linear combinatory algebras, that is, linear combinatory
algebras equipped with a comonadic applicative endomorphism allowing one to construct linear-non-linear
adjunctions between assemblies over a BCI-algebra and a partial combinatory algebra and, thus, describe realisability
semantics for intuitionistic linear logic. Developing the realisability interpretation for

{\bf SILL}(\lambda)_{3}
warrant further research.

[
Hos07
]
, developing some ideas from
[
AHS02
]
introduces linear combinatory and

relational
linear combinatory algebras, that is, linear combinatory
algebras equipped with a comonadic applicative endomorphism allowing one to construct linear-non-linear
adjunctions between assemblies over a BCI-algebra and a partial combinatory algebra and, thus, describe realisability
semantics for intuitionistic linear logic. Developing the realisability interpretation for

{\bf SILL}(\lambda)_{3}
warrant further research since the realisability semantics
would refine the computational motivation standing behind subexponentials.

We have been investigating simply typed linear lambda calculi with subexponential types, but
the determing of whether expanding dependent linear type theory in the fashion of, say,
[
Vák14
]

with subexponentials is of interest as well, but it also deserves a separate and thorough research.
